# Amendments r — world growth: formation, living physics, ecology, the requirement solver, the generation surface, the dossier seam, sail routing, ruins by age (owner rules F1–F9)

Written by the Fable design seat amendments/world-growth-r on 2026-10-23 under the owner's delegation and the J392 model
ruling (`docs/PROGRESS.md` J392: Fable seats design, Opus seats read and apply). The integrating statement this file
builds from is the spine, `docs/design/spine-world-growth-2026-10-23.md` (the shape, the names, the precedence, the
lifetimes and the seams are its rulings; this file designs them to V13's depth and re-decides none); its research
inputs are `research/world/formation-ecology-science.md` (the science, cited "science §n") and
`research/world/formation-ecology-state.md` (the exact current state with line numbers, cited "state Part X / §n").
The spine's §10 and §11 (the atlas and the relational style, F10–F16) are the V15 seat's; this file carries F1–F9 into
V13, the base, V09, the proposal, the brief, INDEX and SUMMARY, and defines the seams V13 owes V15 (the spine's §14).

Numbered from **926** (file q ended at 925).

**PENDING APPLICATION** — no item below is applied; the application seat applies every fragment in order to its

**How to read an item.** As files l and m's: each item names its target file's section (the heading above it), its
title in bold, the owner rule and the spine section it serves (*Requests*), the current text quoted **once** (every
quote verified by exact search against the target file as it now stands on 2026-10-23, and verified again by applying
every item of this file in order to a copy of each target: each quote occurs exactly once at its turn and each new
text exactly once after — the residue check of file m; a quote that wraps across lines is written on one line with
each line break marked ⏎, the continuation's indentation kept), and the new text **in full** as a blockquote whose
lines are the new lines (the `> ` prefix removed; a bare `>` an empty line; a new line that is itself a blockquote in
the target is written `> > …`). An item with several fragments gives each fragment's current and new text in turn,
(a), (b) …, applied in that order. A reference written "file r item n" inside a new text is to this file. *Evidence*
names the spine's sections, the research notes' sections and the sources they cite (host paths relative to the host
repository's root; map paths to this repository's). Order: the owner-rule items, then volume 13 in section order,
then volume 9, the base, the proposal, the brief, INDEX and SUMMARY.

**The clean-room rule for the new texts.** The base and the volumes are implementer inputs: the new texts entering
them name no host source file, function, product or internal identifier and no other product's identifiers — the host
is "the host", the reference generator "the reference generator"; the host's facts are named by the port's field
names (`PolityFacts`, `TravelLaw`, `CampaignFacts`, `SettlementFacts`) and the adapter tables' vocabulary (its terrain
words, route words and relation words travel as data through the host-line adapter folder's tables); the science's
published laws are stated as this volume's own rows with their anchors, the sources staying in this file's *Evidence*
lines. The host's document names, ruling ids, product names and code citations stay in each item's *Evidence* line. The
owner's own words, quoted verbatim in V13 §1.3 (item 939), keep the product name the owner wrote. The proposal and the
brief are research-side and owner-facing documents and may name the products. Checked by a lint over every new text
bound for the base and the volumes (host identifiers, product names, ruling ids, host document names: no hit).

**Where the spine's text was not taken verbatim** (each a design choice of this file, recorded so it can be vetoed):
(a) **the formation grid is 4 × 4 fine cells, not the climate's coarse grid** — the spine says "the coarse raster";
the science note's §11 budgets a formation grid twice the coarse grid's resolution (64 k / 256 k / 576 k cells) inside
the time and memory lines, and ranges and valleys at 2 km (realm) read better than at 4 km; the climate stays on the
8 × 8 coarse grid as the spine says (`formationRatio` 4, a size-row cell); (b) **the realm rung's formation is a
regional tectonic context** (one or two boundaries plus inherited dead belts), the full crust-block model the continent
rung's, and the province rung's one regional feature — the science note's §1.1 finding (a realm holds one active
boundary or none), refining the spine's §3 wording, not its ruling; (c) **the relief-edit dirty set is the whole
monthly march on the coarse grid**, not a downwind strip — the science note's §4.6 finding (a rain shadow persists
200–700 km, most of a realm; the whole march costs 0.2–0.4 s), which satisfies the spine's "downwind until the moisture
is spent or the edge" more simply and exactly; currents re-solve per connected ocean basin; (d) **the monsoon is a
world-context knob at the realm and province rungs** (a continent beyond the map: its side, size and latitude), never
emergent there, and emergent only at the continent rung — the science note's §4.3; (e) **land cover is a cover plate,
not a lens row and not a `WorldRaster` field**: V13 §5.8 rules a lens is never a field over land and is read by no
stage, so the campaign-time layer is its own derived world layer in a cover cache beside the plate cache
(`Document.coverPlates`, BCR-V13-41), outside every digest, read by no stage of W0–W9, by the view, `worldFactsAt` and
the `physicalChange` offer only — the state note's Part E (i); (f) **the formation payload rides the op** as content-
hashed `fields` chunks the op references (the `image` op's form), so J-V13.6's one-input rule holds and a share code
carries the payload hash, re-fetched, never re-run — the state note's Part E (ii); (g) **the steady-state fixed point**
(5–15 linear-time iterations over the lifted hydro core) is the erosion solver, the transient 200-step run never —
the science note's §2.1 and §11; (h) **the erosion refusal is spelled `refuse.world.recipeOp`** everywhere (§3.2's
`refuse.world.op` corrected); (i) **the erode op is retired** rather than un-reserved: erosion runs inside the
formation op and the physics pass's `weather` depth, both baked, never live, so an op named `erode` would be a second
spelling of the `weather` op; (j) **the dossier update is a request the host executes** after the user's approval
(a port message pair, `requestDossierUpdate` / `dossierUpdated`), shown done only on the host's confirmation — the base's
"the module never writes to the dossier" kept whole (the state note's Part E (v)); (k) **the ruins-by-age depth law is
V04's** (`formerRiseCmPerCentury` 60 cm per century, capped at `formerRiseMaxCm` 900), scaled by a material factor from
the science note's anchors (stone-and-brick 0.3–0.5 m, mud brick 0.5–2 m per century), never a second law (the state
note's Part E (vi)); (l) **soil fertility enters W6 through the existing `habitability` term** (the biome row's
habitability × the soil class's fertility), not as a ninth generic term, so J-V13.51's eight terms and V14's R-V14-3
stand; (m) **`WorldRequest.options?` is replaced by `requirements`**, the named fields kept as sugar for `set`
requirements (the state note's Part E (ix)); (n) **the owner's "region map" is the province rung** — a vocabulary row,
no new size row (the state note's Part E (viii)); (o) **the ecology stage is named `WORLD_COVER`**, a fold step with a
stage id like `WORLD_PLACE` and `WORLD_CASCADE`, not a stage of §3.0's W-order (it runs per plate at the lens fold and
at each dated disturbance); (p) **the solver's back-step draws on `world.recipe`** with ordinal keys (the spine's
§4.5), so no new stream is added for it; the one new stream is `world.formation`.

*Not items* (listed so nothing is re-found): the atlas (F11–F15) and the relational style (F10) are V15's (the spine's
§10, §11, §13); INDEX and SUMMARY's V15 rows are the seam seat's (from V15 §10) — this file touches only V13's rows
there; the spine's §12 vocabulary is reconciled by the seam seat (this file uses it: *crust block*, *plate* for the lens
cache's snapshot, *biome* for the climate-level class, *land cover* for the campaign-time layer, *override*,
*disturbance*, *scenario*, *continuation*, *fit*); V04's `buried` kind is cited and unchanged (V04 §3.5.5 (b): the
depth law its cells carry serves the age strata); V14 is unchanged — the physics pass's frame invariant (never a placed
frame's interior nor a free-standing site's footprint) is stated in V13 §4.1 and V14 reads it from there, a V14 item
only if V14's own text needs the rule; `plat/packages/kernel` is untouched (every registry row is appended after M0
under K6, BCR-V13-43); the host's code is read as evidence only (the state note's Part D) and nothing of it is designed.

## The owner's rules on world growth (2026-10-17 to 2026-10-23; quoted verbatim, typos kept; the readings are the session's, vetoable)

**926. OWNER RULE (F1) — Tectonics only in the formation of the world, then deleted for full customization (owner,
2026-10-17 to 2026-10-22; `docs/PROGRESS.md` J390).** "I want to ask you. I know that we are currently not going
tectonic plates mostly for budget reasons, but since we are likely not limiting ourselves to 300 ms like watabou, why
not? Abd then, the whole idea as that tectonics are only used in the formation of the world and anything else to help
build biomes emulating real world. After which it's deleted for full customization for the user. What are your
thoughts?"

  **(a) THE 300 MS WAS THE REFERENCE GENERATOR'S HEIGHTMAP BUDGET, NOT OURS.** This design's one-click budgets are the
  size rows' (V13 §2.3, §6: 900 / 3 000 / 7 000 ms) inside the base's ten-second comfort level (§12), and a formation
  step of 0.3–2.3 s fits beside them (science §11; item 983). **(b) FORMATION IS THE FIRST RECIPE OPERATION, RUN ONCE.**
  A `formation` op opens a recipe that asks for a scenario (item 948, item 961): crust blocks, uplift and subsidence,
  fluvial erosion by the steady-state stream-power fixed point over the lifted hydro core, hillslope relaxation,
  glacial carving where cold, a sea-level history and soil formation, all on the formation grid. **(c) ITS RESULTS ARE
  BAKED, ITS STATE DELETED.** The op keeps only its results — the terrain it produced, a bedrock raster, a soil raster
  and named landforms as entities — as ordinary editable data; the block set, the velocities, the boundaries, the
  solver's state and every intermediate are discarded; a saved world never re-runs formation on load, a formation code
  change moves no stored world (a version pin and T-V13-57), and a share code carries the payload's hash, re-fetched,
  never re-run (item 958, item 988). **(d) FULL CUSTOMIZATION.** Everything formation made is ordinary data under the
  recipe's later ops, the DM's brushes and the two verbs (items 961, 975); templates stay for authored control, the
  scenarios join them as rows (J-V13.41 reversed by J-V13.107, its reason kept as the authored path). **(e) THE
  PROVINCE RUNG INHERITS ONE REGIONAL FEATURE; THE REALM RUNG A REGIONAL TECTONIC CONTEXT.** A realm-sized map holds
  one active boundary or none (science §1.1), so the full crust-block model is the continent rung's (item 961).

**927. OWNER RULE (F2) — Biome formation simulated too; the simulation deleted but with dynamism (owner, J390).**
"What about how all of this relates to biome formation? We would want that to be simulated as well then the u deeming
simulation deleted but have dynamism."

  **(a) THREE LIFETIMES, ONE PER LAYER** (the spine's §3). *Formation* (F1) runs once and is deleted. *Living physics*
  — monthly climate with seasonal wind belts, the monsoon term, ocean currents and their coastal effect, hydrology,
  potential natural vegetation — is law, never history: re-derived on every generation and on every edit over its dirty
  set, never baked (items 962, 964, 971). *Campaign-time ecology* — land cover = potential vegetation × human use ×
  disturbance × succession — runs from dated host facts and dated DM events only, as a pure function of (the potential
  vegetation, the cell's last dated event, the weeks since), never stepped and never random (science §6.3; item 959's
  `WORLD_COVER`, item 954's `CoverPlate`). **(b) THE DYNAMISM IS THE LIVING PHYSICS AND THE ECOLOGY, NOT THE
  SIMULATION.** A raised range dries the land downwind (the whole monthly march re-run, item 971); a settlement's
  population clears fields by travel time from its seat; an abandoned, razed or depopulated seat's fields regrow by the
  biome's succession table; a fire scars and recovers — all without plates, all re-derivable from the recipe and the
  event log. **(c) LAND COVER IS NOT THE DOSSIER'S BIOME.** The dossier holds the climate-level biome (V11's
  `ClimateRow`); clearing and regrowth are land cover; only a climate-level change can raise the conflict list (item 969),
  ecology never (T-V13-62). **(d) THE CLASSIFICATION IS DATA.** W5 classifies monthly climate, soil and water by a
  published-style threshold table (`world.koppen`) into potential natural vegetation (`world.pnv`); the biome matrix stays
  as the import's derive-only path (item 964).

**928. OWNER RULE (F3) — A let-physics-pass-again option; the DM always wins; settlement age decides the underlying
ruins (owner, J390).** "Okay then with this add a let physics pass again option as well for the whole world of select
areas: The DM always wins. A painted biome is a "this was always so" override that the physics respects and builds
around. A painted fire is a "this happened" event that the land recovers from. Also, settlement age (generation only)
also determines if and how large an underlying ruins would be."

  **(a) THE TWO VERBS ON EVERY PHYSICAL LAYER** (the spine's §7.1). A painted biome, land cover, soil or current is an
  **always-so override**: a boundary condition of every dirty set, never overwritten by a re-derivation, its
  neighbours derived against it (item 954's `Override`, item 971). A painted fire, flood, clearing or eruption is a
  **this-happened disturbance**: dated, the land recovers by the succession tables, never undone by physics, never
  erased by a reroll (item 954's `Disturbance`, item 975's `disturb`). **(b) THE PHYSICS PASS** is one command,
  `physicsPass`, over the whole world, a region or one layer, at three depths — *rederive* (the living physics with
  every override held), *release* (the DM's always-so overrides in scope released, each kept as visual history or
  removed, the DM's choice) and *weather* (formation's slow processes over the current terrain for a chosen span of
  years, baked as a `weather` op and deleted again) — always on the counterfactual fork with preview and commit (items
  975, 978). **(c) ITS INVARIANTS.** It never touches a placed settlement frame's interior nor a free-standing site's
  footprint (the blend band holds); host-bound facts are offered (`physicalChange`), never imposed; this-happened
  events are kept; a dossier contradiction raises the conflict list (item 975). **(d) RUINS BY AGE, GENERATION ONLY.**
  On the settlement side (the base), the settlement's age — `HistoryHint.founding.yearsAgo`, else the tier's fallback —
  decides at generation whether earlier strata lie beneath it, how large (a share of the oldest growth stages' area,
  growing with age and tier, bounded) and how deep (V04's accumulation law × the age, scaled by material, capped),
  composed with the scar burial and the buried former settlement (union of extents, the deepest floor wins), a
  deterministic draw keyed by the town root, never grown during the campaign (items 994–997).

**929. OWNER RULE (F4) — Ocean currents for sail routes and speed (owner, 2026-10-22; J391).** "Oh what about ocean
currents for sail routes and speed?"

  **(a) THE LIVING PHYSICS HAS CURRENTS.** W3 solves a coarse gyre per connected ocean basin from the monthly wind
  stress and the coast geometry — western boundary currents, equatorial currents, the gyres — with a coastal
  temperature and precipitation effect (a cold upwelling coast is a desert coast); a strait opened or closed re-solves
  its basin (item 962). **(b) EVERY SEA LEG IS PRICED BY DIRECTION, MONTH AND VESSEL KIND.** `world.sailing` holds the
  vessel kinds and their polars (speed against the wind angle), the current coupling, tacking and leeway, and the month
  sets; W7's water legs are chosen per direction and season — outbound and return may differ, sailing windows emerge
  (item 966's §3.8 (12), item 951's `WorldRoute.sailing`). **(c) DETERMINISTIC, NO RANDOM WEATHER.** Storms stay the
  host's storm season; every effort is a pure function of the live wind and current fields and the coast. **(d) THE
  HOST'S LANE STAYS SYMMETRIC UNTIL IT ADOPTS DIRECTION.** `TravelEdge` is unchanged; the port offers directional,
  seasonal sea efforts beside the host's symmetric lanes (H33) and sends the symmetric mean until the host adopts them;
  a lens drawing a sea link uses the host's own leg unless the host adopted ours (items 970, 982). **(e) A STRAIT EDIT
  REPORTS ITS BAND CROSSINGS**, never silently (item 977).

**930. OWNER RULE (F5) — A world generated first, then editable; preconfiguration options random all / basic /
advanced for all user types (owner, 2026-10-22 to 2026-10-23; J392).** "With this, like the Settlementforge map
generation, a world map should already be generated then made edit able by a player. But there should be
preconfiguration options (random all, basic, advanced) for all user types. What are your thoughts and how should that
be filled out?"

  **(a) GENERATED FIRST.** Opening a world page shows a generated world at once; the panel's options steer the NEXT
  run; in a campaign the settlements are fixed inputs (item 992). **(b) ONE LAYERED PANEL, NOT MODES.** *Random all*
  (one button, the seed shown) · *Basic*, always visible (size; shape — the templates and the formation scenarios;
  climate band; land and sea; ruggedness; wetness; settlement density on a standalone world) · *Advanced*, collapsible
  groups each with random and reset (terrain; formation; climate; water; biomes with the inclusion chips; resources;
  routes and sites; politics on a standalone world; names) · *Deep constraints* (sketch landmasses or mountain lines;
  pin a feature; import a heightmap or a legacy map; lock regions and regenerate the rest). V09 owns the UI (items
  990–993); V13 owns the semantics (item 960): **every option has three states** — random / set / locked — every
  option defaults to random, and the user's choices (with their random sentinels) are stored apart from the rolled
  facts, regeneration re-reading the choices, never the rolled facts. **(c) FOR ALL USER TYPES: NOTHING HIDDEN.** Every
  control is drawn for every account kind, disabled with its reason where an account kind may not use it; the gate is
  enforced at the one generate action, never in the panel (item 992). Who gets which level is the owner's (Q5, item
  988). **(d) HOW IT IS FILLED OUT.** By the one requirement solver (F9): each option is a requirement of the run.

**931. OWNER RULE (F6) — The inclusion of biomes, organic in when and how they are simulated (owner, J392).** "Or the
inclusions of biomes so that when and how they're simulated is organic."

  **(a) AN INCLUSION IS A REQUIREMENT, NEVER PAINT.** "This world includes a desert / a glacier / a rainforest, about
  so much" is a `Requirement` of kind `biome` with an amount (item 954), met by the solver through the climate's causes
  — the latitude box, a range upwind, a basin without outlet, a cold current's coast, a narrowed sea — never by writing
  biome cells (item 960's W0 and the back-step of item 959). **(b) ORGANIC IN WHEN AND HOW.** The solver meets it at
  the stage best placed to (W0 widens the latitude box when it is random; formation raises the range; W3 verifies and
  sends one bounded back-step to W1 for the smallest cause); the result is a biome where the physics explains it, at a
  plausible extent, with graded edges (T-V13-64's "no arid dot").

**932. OWNER RULE (F7) — "Based off of my settlements"; a best-placement button; organic self-correction, never an
arid dot (owner, J392).** "Or a simple configuration called "based off of my settlements" as in they make a campaign
and have settlements saved so to match and to create dynamic scenarios, just matches a world map to appropriately
include everything that is necessary for those settlements, that and an optional button that says allows a user to
automatically place a settlement into a preexisting map in a best placement. Also as it comes to biomes, if we place a
settlement with dossier arid biome into a map that doesn't have an arid biome, the map has to self correct but that
shouldn't just be a random arid dot in the middle. What are your thoughts or how would you refine?"

  **(a) THE SAME SOLVER AT THREE SCALES** (the spine's §4). *Based off of my settlements:* the campaign's settlements'
  dossier facts and relationships, read through the port (`CampaignFacts`: terrain word, coast and its side, river and
  inflow, lake, climate row, latitude, resources, size and tier, trade access; neighbours, partners, rivals, overlords
  and vassals, isolation — H31), enter the run as requirements of rank 1; W0 lays their **planned sites** first from
  their relationships, and the stages grow the world around them (item 960). *Best placement:* `placeFrame` with
  `best: true` resumes the same process at the placement stage with one more requirement under the least-change goal,
  searches the whole map and offers the three best with reasons (item 967). *Organic self-correction:* only when no
  site is lawful does it step back into W3 or W1 for the smallest causal change — a minimum extent by rung, graded
  edges, every other settlement's facts protected, the new country explaining itself ("the rain shadow of the Greyspine")
  — and painting is the labelled last resort, only when the DM forbids terrain change (item 967; T-V13-64). **(b) THE
  THREE MECHANISMS ARE ONE.** W6's scoring and P1's coherence are subsumed by the solver (W6 reads the included
  settlements' requirements, `nearestLawful` searches the whole map); the host's one-button placer becomes a host row that
  calls `placeFrame`'s best form (H31; the state note's Part E (iv)). **(c) THE DOSSIER'S ARID IS A REQUIREMENT, NOT A
  DOT.** The dossier's terrain word resolves to a climate row (DR42) and enters as a `biome` requirement; an unmet one
  after the search is the back-step's cause, never a cell painted around the town.

**933. OWNER RULE (F8) — A DM may author a different biome one day: a warning or pop-up; the dossier changes only
after the user's approval (owner, J392).** "Now, we also have to consider that the DM may author a different biome one
day. In this case, a warning or pop up, and/ir it changes the dossier fact only after user approval from the warning."

  **(a) THE CONFLICT LIST IS THE POP-UP** (the spine's §5.3). `W_EDIT_DOSSIER` is generalised into `W_CONFLICT`: a
  conflict names every affected settlement and offers, everywhere, the same choices — cancel; fit the world or paint
  around it; keep the mismatch (flagged); update the dossier, in one of two kinds ("the land changed", a dated host
  event, the default; "it was always so", the host's retcon path); move the town (never under `hostLocked`); change
  the request (at generation) (item 969, item 975). **(b) THE DOSSIER CHANGES ONLY ON THE USER'S APPROVAL, AND ONLY
  THROUGH THE HOST'S OWN COMMAND.** The map sends `requestDossierUpdate` through the port; the host carries it out by
  its own world-fact command after the approval and answers `dossierUpdated`; the map shows it done only on that
  confirmation (`consumed`'s form); each side keeps its own undo; the module writes no dossier fact (item 970, item 998;
  H32). **(c) THE LADDER DECIDES WHEN IT ASKS** (the spine's §5.1–§5.2): dossier facts > the DM's overrides and locks >
  the user's requests > physical plausibility > randomness; it asks exactly when an action would change something owned
  by a higher rank than its own, never about ranks 4 and 5, so "Random all" keeps its one click (conflicts resolve to the
  safe defaults, summarised). **(d) PRESETS PRE-DECIDE MAP-SIDE CHOICES ONLY**; updating a dossier always asks;
  decisions are remembered with their requirement and re-asked only when what they rested on changed (item 954's
  `Decision`).

**934. OWNER RULE (F9) — All coherent and refined; the generation surface and the one requirement solver apply to
the same situation or in sequence, and are one process where appropriate (owner, J392).** "Now I threw a lot of pieces
at you. This should be all coherent and refined, tell me your thoughts." then "The one generation surface feature needs
to also cohere with the one authority and approval model. Both options should apply to the same situation or in
sequence." then "That was wrong I meant One requirement solver not the one authority." then "But they should also be one
process where appropriate."

  **(a) THE ENGINE IS THE PIPELINE.** There is no separate solver program: W0 → W9 and P1–P5 are the solver — each
  stage satisfies the requirements it is best placed to satisfy as it builds and verifies them as it produces; W3's
  one bounded back-step to W1 (`solver.maxBackSteps` 3, each on the coarse rung) is the only loop; every correction is
  an ordinary recipe op with `origin: 'solver'` and the requirement it serves, so the recipe stays the one authoring
  history and a world re-derives from it without the solver; the run ends with a verification report (item 959, 960).
  **(b) AT ONCE AND IN SEQUENCE.** The panel's options and the included settlements' requirements enter one run;
  placing a settlement later resumes the same process at the placement stage; an option change re-runs from the
  earliest stage it affects; an approval point pauses the run and it continues after the decision; hand placement is
  checked by the same stages in a dry run and never authored for them. **(c) ONE PROCESS.** The panel's live preview is
  the coarse run of these exact stages; Generate runs them at the fine rung; same requirements, same decisions, same
  seed → the same world by either path (T-V13-64). **(d) ONE INTERFACE.** `solve(requirements, state, goal)` in
  `@plat/world/solve` is the engine's one entry (item 943, 954); V15 calls it over map placements (the spine's §14.5).

### Volume 13 — §0 In plain words

**937.** **What the world is, and is not, over time; the world grows around its towns; the game master always wins.**
*Requests:* F1, F2, F3, F7, F9 (the spine's §3, §4, §7).
*Current text:*
> **What the world is not.** It does not move continents, erode valleys over ages, simulate currents or wrap round a⏎globe; it does not run the campaign's wars, trade or religions — the host does that and the world only draws where⏎those facts fall. Maps made with the old generator can be brought in once as data, and the world can be written out in⏎the shapes that generator's tools expect, so nothing the campaign already has is lost.
*New text:*
> **What the world is, and is not, over time.** Its continents moved and its valleys were cut once, at its birth: a
> formation step raises ranges where crust blocks meet, cuts rivers into them over imagined ages, lets ice carve the
> cold valleys and the sea stand high and low, and lays soils on the result — then it bakes what it made (the ground,
> the rock beneath, the soils, the named ranges, rifts, volcanoes and fjords) as ordinary data and forgets how: no crust block
> ever moves again, nothing of the simulation is kept, and everything it made is the game master's to reshape. Its
> weather, its currents, its rivers and the vegetation its climate would grow are **living physics**, re-derived
> whenever the ground changes — raise a range and the land behind it dries, open a strait and the currents turn. What
> the land looks like *today* — the fields around a town, a forest regrowing on a razed village's land, a burn scar —
> follows from dated facts alone: the campaign's settlements and what happened to them, never from a clock of the
> map's own and never from dice. It does not wrap round a globe; it does not run the campaign's wars, trade or
> religions — the host does that and the world only draws where those facts fall. Maps made with the old generator can
> be brought in once as data, and the world can be written out in the shapes that generator's tools expect, so nothing
> the campaign already has is lost.
>
> **The world grows around its towns.** Ask for a world "based off of my settlements" and the campaign's towns are
> not dropped onto a map that happens to exist: their facts — a port on a south coast, a river town, a desert city, a
> mountain hold, who trades with whom and who is at war — are the requirements the world is grown to meet, the ranges
> rising where rain shadows are needed and the coast forming where ports need it, so every town lands where its own
> dossier says it belongs. Place a town later and the same machinery resumes: it searches the whole map for the best
> lawful spot and offers three; only when none exists does it change the land — by the smallest cause that explains the
> change, at a plausible size, with graded edges, every other town's facts protected — never by painting a desert
> around a town. Every option on the generation panel is one more such requirement, random unless you set it, locked
> if you say so; a live preview runs the same stages at a coarse scale as you choose.
>
> **The game master always wins.** Paint a biome, a soil or a current and it is *always so*: the physics respects it
> and derives its neighbours against it. Paint a fire, a flood, a clearing or an eruption and *this happened*: the land
> recovers from it at the pace its biome recovers. Let physics pass again over the whole world or a chosen area —
> re-deriving around your overrides, releasing them, or weathering the ground for an imagined span of years — and see
> the whole consequence on a side copy before you commit. Where a change would contradict what a campaign's town says
> about itself, the map asks, listing every town it touches and the choices; the town's own record changes only on your
> approval, and only through the host that keeps it.
*Evidence:* the spine's §3 (three lifetimes), §4 (one solver), §7 (the DM always wins); the paragraph's own form (§0 is the owner-facing summary).

### Volume 13 — §2.1 What the base already declares, and the two regimes

**945.** **The decree law names the physics pass and the dossier update.**
*Requests:* F3, F8 (the spine's §5.3, §7.2).
*Current text:*
> a retcon, a regenerate, a size or climate `adjust` only as a geometry shift the host adopts by its own⏎receipted re-canonize; political and military event kinds are refused (`refuse.world.hostPolitical`), the world
*New text:*
> a retcon, a regenerate, a physics pass (a release of an override or a `weather` op among them), a size or climate
> `adjust` only as a geometry shift the host adopts by its own receipted re-canonize; a dossier update is never a map
> write — the conflict list's `requestDossierUpdate` rides the port and the host's own world-fact command carries it out
> after the user's approval, the map showing it done only on the host's `dossierUpdated` (V13 §3.11.5 (1), §3.11.6);
> political and military event kinds are refused (`refuse.world.hostPolitical`), the world
*Evidence:* the spine's §5.3 ("the map sends it through the host's own world-fact command and shows it done only on the host's confirmation"), §7.3 (the three verbs' table); the state note's A.11 (the decree law), D.7 (the host's world-fact card and its guard-and-override pattern).


### Volume 13 — §2.2 New records

**946.** **`WorldRecipe` carries the choices, the requirements, the decisions, the included campaign, the world context and the edge charters.**
*Requests:* F5–F9 (the spine's §4.1, §5.4, §6, §10.4).
*Current text:*
>   recipe: RecipeOp[] /* the terrain recipe, W1 (V13 §3.2): the world's authoring history, every op seeded and stored */;
*New text:*
>   recipe: RecipeOp[] /* the terrain recipe, W1 (V13 §3.2): the world's authoring history, every op seeded and stored; a `formation` op first where a scenario was asked (V13 §3.2.1); the solver's corrections (`origin: 'solver'`) and the physics pass's `weather` ops appended in their turn (V13 §3.1 (5), §4.1) */;
>   choices: OptionChoice[] /* the world dialog's options with their strengths — every option the panel shows, `random` unless set or locked — stored APART from what was rolled: a regeneration re-reads the choices, never the rolled facts (V13 §3.1 (1); J-V13.123) */;
>   requirements: Requirement[] /* the run's requirements (V13 §3.1 (5)): the choices resolved, the biome inclusions, the included settlements' facts and relationships, the DM's locks and overrides, the edge charters, the host's water verdicts; the solver's input and the verification report's rows */;
>   decisions: Decision[] /* the conflicts decided, each with its requirement and what it rested on (V13 §3.11.5 (1)); reused by rerolls and physics passes, re-asked only when what it rested on changed */;
>   include?: { campaign: string; settlements: string[] /* host keys */; version: number /* the `CampaignFacts` window the facts were read at */ } /* "based off of my settlements" (V13 §3.1 (5)): the campaign whose settlements' facts entered as rank-1 requirements; absent on a standalone world */;
>   worldContext?: { monsoon?: { side: 'N'|'E'|'S'|'W'; sizeKm: number; latDeg: number } } /* the world beyond the map that the living physics reads but never makes below the continent rung (V13 §3.4 (5)): a continent beyond the edge whose summer heating pulls the monsoon in; a `set` or `locked` requirement of kind `context`, never emergent at the realm or province rung */;
>   edgeCharters?: { side: 'N'|'E'|'S'|'W'; neighbour: { doc: Uuid; version: string }; charter: WorldEdgeCharter }[] /* a continuation (V13 §3.1 (6)): the atlas neighbours' edge charters this world was generated from, stored whole for provenance as a frame stores its charter (≤ a few hundred KB per edge, V13 §2.7); never re-read after generation — nothing dated crosses a seam */;
*Evidence:* the spine's §4.1 ("Every requirement is a record in the world document (`Recipe.world.requirements`), with the decisions taken on its conflicts beside it"), §6 (the choices with their `random` sentinels stored apart from what was rolled), §10.4 (the charters stored with each neighbour's version); the science note's §4.3 (a realm cannot make a monsoon); the state note's A.16 (`WorldRecipe` holds no requirement, preset or seam context).

**947.** **`RecipeOp`: the `formation` and `weather` ops with their baked payloads; the `erode` op retired; every op's provenance.**
*Requests:* F1, F3 (the spine's §3, §7.3).
*Current text:*
>   | { op: 'erode'; passes: number /* reserved, shipped absent from every template (J-V13.42) */ };
*New text:*
>   | { op: 'formation'; scenario: string /* a `world.formation` scenario row key (V13 §2.3) */; knobs: FormationKnobs /* the row's knobs as drawn or set (V13 §3.2.1) */; seedIx: number;
>       baked: { version: string /* `formation.version`: the solver's pin — a payload is read under the version it was baked with and never re-run under another (J-V13.111; `W_FORMATION_VERSION` when the library's is newer, informative) */; grid: { cellCm: Cm; cols: number; rows: number } /* the formation grid: `fineCellCm` × the size row's `formationRatio` */;
>                dem: ChunkRef[] /* the formation-grid DEM, Int32 cm, content-hashed `fields` chunks (V13 §2.7) */; bedrock: ChunkRef; soil: ChunkRef; surfaceAge: ChunkRef /* Uint16, log-years: the soil law's time input, kept for the `weather` op */; landforms: Id[] /* the `Landform` entities minted at the bake */;
>                stats: { landShare: number; hackH: number; bifurcation: number } /* the bake's own report (T-V13-58) */ } /* the op's RESULT, baked at W1's first run of it; the block set, the velocities, the boundaries, the distance fields, the erosion solver's state and every intermediate are deleted at the bake and exist in no digest (T-V13-57) */ }
>   | { op: 'weather'; years: number; scope: { kind: 'world' } | { kind: 'region'; box: { c0: number; r0: number; c1: number; r1: number } }; seedIx: number;
>       baked: { version: string; dem: ChunkRef[] /* the formation-grid delta the slow processes made over the ground as it stood, applied by W1 after the ops before it */; soil: ChunkRef; landforms: Id[] } };   // the physics pass's `weather` depth (V13 §4.1): formation's erosion, hillslope and soil processes with uplift 0 over the current terrain for `years`, baked once on the fork and carried by `commitFork`; never a live process
>   // every op may carry `origin?: 'template'|'user'|'solver'|'physicsPass'|'import'` (absent: as the event's author says) and `requirement?: Id` (the requirement a solver's correction serves, V13 §3.1 (5)) — provenance read by the clerk and the verification report, never by W1; no `erode` op exists (J-V13.108: erosion runs only inside `formation` and `weather`, both baked, never live)
*Evidence:* the spine's §3 ("its RESULTS, baked into the recipe as the formation op's payload"; "a change to the formation code never shifts a stored world"), §7.3 (*let time pass*: "baked and deleted again"); the science note's §2.1 (*Bake or live*: the weather depth with U = 0; "at 0.1 mm/yr for 10 kyr the land lowers about 1 m"), §11; the state note's A.3 (the reserved `erode` op and its two refusal spellings), E (ii).

**948.** **`WorldRaster`: `geology` derived from `bedrock`; the formation grid, `bedrock` and `soil`; the monthly climate, the maritime fraction, the currents and the basins.**
*Requests:* F1, F2, F4 (the spine's §3).
*Current text* (a):
>   geology: Uint8Array /* fine; 0 ordinary, 1 soluble, 2 volcanic, 3 impermeable — written by the recipe's geology ops, default 0 */;
*New text* (a):
>   geology: Uint8Array /* fine; 0 ordinary, 1 soluble, 2 volcanic, 3 impermeable — derived from `bedrock` by `world.formation`'s bedrock→geology row where a formation op ran (V13 §3.2.1 (8)), overwritten inside a `geology` op's disc or band, default 0 on a world with neither */;
>   formation?: { cellCm: Cm; cols: number; rows: number; fineToFormation: Uint32Array /* per fine cell its formation cell (the index map) */ } /* the formation grid: fine × `formationRatio` (4); present exactly when the recipe holds a `formation` or `weather` op */;
>   bedrock: Uint8Array /* FORMATION grid; `world.formation`'s bedrock classes (0 ordinary sedimentary, 1 shield, 2 folded belt, 3 arc volcanics, 4 flood basalt, 5 limestone, 6 evaporite, 7 alluvium, 8 till, 9 ash, 10 young lava): the formation op's bake; read by the resource rules, the soil law, the charter's `bedrock` and the view; `geology` is its four-class reading; all 0 on a world with no formation op */;
>   soil: Uint8Array /* FORMATION grid; `world.soil`'s classes (V13 §2.3): the formation op's bake, re-baked by a `weather` op; read by W5's overrides and resource rules, W6's habitability, the cover fold's fertility and drainage, the charter's `soil`; a fine cell's class is its formation cell's with the fine-scale overrides of V13 §3.2.1 (9) applied at read time (a slope above 30 %, an active floodplain), so no fine soil raster is ever resident */;
*Current text* (b):
>   climate: { tempDeciC: Int16Array; seasonAmpDeciC: Int16Array; seasonPhaseWeek: Uint8Array; precipMm: Uint16Array; evapMm: Uint16Array;⏎             windCdeg: Uint16Array; windStrength: Uint8Array /* 0–255 → 0–1 */; continentality: Uint8Array /* 0–255 → 0–1 */ } /* coarse; W3 */;
*New text* (b):
>   climate: { tempDeciC: Int16Array; seasonAmpDeciC: Int16Array; seasonPhaseWeek: Uint8Array; precipMm: Uint16Array; evapMm: Uint16Array;
>              windCdeg: Uint16Array; windStrength: Uint8Array /* 0–255 → 0–1 */; continentality: Uint8Array /* 0–255 → 0–1 */ /* the annual summaries every existing reader keeps reading: the mean and the first-harmonic fit of the twelve months (V13 §3.4 (4)), the annual sum, the annual-mean wind, the maritime fraction's complement */;
>              tempMonthDeciC: Int16Array[] /* twelve arrays, coarse: the month's mean temperature, month 0 the host calendar's first month (V13 §2.3's phase rows) */; precipMonthMm: Uint16Array[] /* twelve: the month's precipitation, Σ = `precipMm` */;
>              windMonthCdeg: Uint16Array[]; windMonthStrength: Uint8Array[] /* twelve each: the month's wind after the belts' seasonal shift and the thermal term (V13 §3.4 (5)) */;
>              maritime: Uint8Array /* 0–255 → 0–1: the air's maritime fraction by upwind fetch over water (V13 §3.4 (3)); `continentality` = 1 − it */;
>              currentMonth: Int8Array[] /* twelve arrays × two lanes (u east, v north), coarse ocean cells, in cm/s ÷ 2 (±127 ≈ ±2.5 m/s): the basin gyre solve (V13 §3.4 (6b)); 0 on land */; sstAnomalyDeciC: Int8Array /* coarse ocean cells: the sea-surface temperature anomaly the currents and the upwelling make against the latitude's mean (V13 §3.4 (2)) */;
>              basin: Uint16Array /* coarse ocean cells: the connected ocean basin id the current solve runs per and a strait edit re-solves per (V13 §3.14.2) */ } /* coarse; W3 */;
*Evidence:* the spine's §3 (the lifetime table: bedrock and soil rasters; monthly temperature and precipitation, seasonal wind belts, monsoon shift, ocean currents and their coastal effect); the science note's §3.3 (soil Uint8), §4.1 (the maritime fraction: "same pass, one extra float"), §4.4 (the Munk width 35–85 km: the coarse grid resolves it at every rung; the solve per connected basin), §5.1 (24 monthly values per cell), §11 (the memory of the monthly arrays ≈ 7 MB + 7 MB at the continent rung); the state note's A.3.

*Current text* (c):

*New text* (c):

>   soil: { mode: string /* a `world.soil` class */; fertility: number /* 0–1, the class row's use attribute */; drainage: 'free'|'moderate'|'poor' } /* the mode over the frame; S0's arable reading (§4.2 S0's demand) may read `fertility` through the charter where the base's rows admit it — a `map`-tier input, never a dossier fact */;

**950.** **`WorldRoute.sailing`: a water route's efforts by vessel, month and direction.**
*Requests:* F4 (the spine's §8).
*Current text:*
>   seasonal?: { iceWeeks?: number[]; highWaterWeeks?: number[]; snowWeeks?: number[] /* 52-week sets keyed to the host's week of year: ice on a water run, high water on a ford, snow on the route's highest segment */ };
*New text:*
>   seasonal?: { iceWeeks?: number[]; highWaterWeeks?: number[]; snowWeeks?: number[] /* 52-week sets keyed to the host's week of year: ice on a water run, high water on a ford, snow on the route's highest segment */ };
>   sailing?: { vessel: 'sail'|'oared'|'coastal'; byMonth: { out: number; back: number /* integer sea effort-metres a → b and b → a (V13 §3.8 (12)): the laid course's length ÷ its ground-speed share, so a leg with the wind and the current costs fewer metres than the same leg against them */;
>     outCourse?: WorldPointCm[]; backCourse?: WorldPointCm[] /* the laid course where it differs from `polyline` (a return by another way: the coast-hugging route home) */; closed?: boolean /* the month set's risk multiplier at its cap: a sea closed in this month (a cost, never a ban) */ }[] /* twelve */ }[] /* a water route only: one row per vessel kind of `world.sailing`; `stations`, `openSeaM`, `coastalM` and the canon's water metres stay the SYMMETRIC MEAN over the twelve months and both directions until the host adopts direction (H33; `TravelEdge` unchanged) */;
*Evidence:* the spine's §8 ("W7's water legs are chosen per direction and season (outbound and return may differ …)"; "sending the symmetric mean until the host adopts direction; the canon's `TravelEdge` stays symmetric unless the host asks"); the science note's §8.5 (Casson's with-and-against ratio 3–7×, the "against" route a different, longer path); the state note's A.16 (`TravelEdge` symmetric: J391's gap).

**951.** **`SettlementPhysical`: the seat's soil, stone, land-cover share and hazard readings as derived facts.**
*Requests:* F1, F2 (the spine's §3; H34).
*Current text:*

*New text:*
> interface SettlementPhysical { elevationM: number; slopePct: number; terrainClass: string; climateBand: string; tempDeciC: DeciC; seasonAmpDeciC: DeciC; precipMm: MmYr; lake?: 'fresh'|'brackish'|'salt'|'dry'; floodSummary?: { wetSeasonShare: number };
>   soil?: { class: string; fertility: number; drainage: string } /* the seat's soil, a `world.soil` class: a derived reading the host's agriculture may read (H34) */; bedrock?: string /* the local stone */;
>   landCoverShare?: Record<string, number> /* the cover classes' shares within the seat's catchment (`cover.catchmentHours`) at the record's week, from the cover plate — a DERIVED reading the host may show beside its own dossier, never a genesis fact and never its biome (H34; V13 §3.6 (5)) */;
>   hazards?: { kind: 'volcano'|'fault'|'flood'|'glacier'; distanceM: number; landform?: Id }[] /* the active landforms within `cover.catchmentHours` of the seat and the wet-season flood share, offered to the host's calamity exposure as readings (H34); never a strike, never a roll */ }   // sampled from the world after the blend
*Evidence:* the spine's §3 (ecology "offered to the host as `physicalChange`"), §7.2 ("host-bound facts are offered, never imposed"); the science note's §1.7 (volcanoes the DM may mark active), §3.2 (the use attributes); the state note's A.13 (`SettlementPhysical`), D.8 (the host's calamity default by terrain, its only terrain-to-hazard law).

**952.** **`WorldFacts` reads the land cover, the soil, the bedrock, the landforms and a sea route's month at a point.**
*Requests:* F2, F4 (the spine's §3, §8).

*New text:*

>   landCover: { class: string; sinceWeek?: Week; event?: Id } /* the cover plate's class at the cell at `week` (`landCoverAt`, V13 §3.6 (5)): what V14's E1 world crop and a placed frame's surroundings draw — a derived reading, never written back, never a stage's input */;
>   soil: string; bedrock: string; landforms: Id[] /* the `Landform`s whose outline holds the cell */; route?: Id; sailing?: { vessel: string; month: number; out: number; back: number } /* a water route at the cell: the month's efforts both ways (V13 §3.8 (12)) */; territory?: Id /* the seat territory */; sites: Id[] /* the site Placements at the cell */;
*Evidence:* the spine's §3 (land cover read "at view time"), §13 (item 132 (e)'s facts at a point); the state note's A.14 (a lens is read by no stage: so the cover reaches V14 as a reading of `worldFactsAt`, not as a lens).

**953.** **The new records: the solver's (`Requirement`, `SettlementFacts`, `CampaignFacts`, `Conflict`, `Decision`, `VerificationReport`, `SolveResult`, `solve`), the DM's (`Override`, `Disturbance`, `CoverPlate`), formation's (`Landform`, `FormationKnobs`), the seams' (`WorldEdgeCharter`, `RenderedWorld`) and sailing's (`SailingLeg`).**
*Requests:* F1–F9; the spine's §3, §4.1, §5.1–§5.4, §7.1, §8, §14.
*Current text:*
>   key: boolean /* a whole plate; else a delta on the previous kept plate */; pinned: boolean }   // the world document's plate cache (`Document.lensPlates`, BCR-V13-38), outside every digest (V13 §2.7, §5.8 (8))
*New text:*
>   key: boolean /* a whole plate; else a delta on the previous kept plate */; pinned: boolean }   // the world document's plate cache (`Document.lensPlates`, BCR-V13-38), outside every digest (V13 §2.7, §5.8 (8))
> // @plat/world/solve — the requirement solver's records (V13 §3.0, §3.1, §3.11; the spine's §4 and §5; J-V13.117 – J-V13.123): the pipeline IS the solver; these are its inputs, its conflicts and its answers
> type Rank = 1|2|3|4|5;   // the precedence ladder (V13 §3.11.5 (1)): 1 a placed or included settlement's dossier facts; 2 the DM's overrides and locks; 3 the user's requests; 4 physical plausibility; 5 randomness
> type Strength = 'random'|'set'|'locked';   // every option's three states (V13 §3.1 (1)); `random` the default of every option
> interface OptionChoice { option: string /* a `world.presets` option key: size, template, scenario, latBox, poles, wind, biomeMatrix, seats, density, ruggedness, wetness, landShare, climateBand, … */; strength: Strength; value?: unknown /* absent under `random`: the sentinel is the absence */; region?: { box: { c0: number; r0: number; c1: number; r1: number } } /* a choice scoped to a region (a locked region, a regional reroll) */ }
> interface Requirement { id: Id; source: 'option'|'inclusion'|'settlement'|'relation'|'lock'|'override'|'edgeCharter'|'hostRequires'|'frame'|'context';
>   rank: Rank; kind: 'extent'|'latBox'|'poles'|'wind'|'template'|'scenario'|'landShare'|'ruggedness'|'wetness'|'biome'|'resource'|'site'|'relation'|'coast'|'river'|'lake'|'climateRow'|'latitude'|'terrain'|'route'|'edge'|'lockRegion'|'overrideLayer'|'water'|'context'|'density'|'polity'|'names';
>   target: unknown /* the kind's value: a biome id with an amount in km² (`inclusion`), a settlement's facts (`SettlementFacts`), a relation (`{ a; b; word; travelDaysMax? }`), an edge charter's station set, a region's cells, an option's value … */;
>   strength: Strength; scope?: { box: { c0: number; r0: number; c1: number; r1: number } } | { landmass: number } | { seat: string }; settlement?: string /* the host key the requirement belongs to (sources `settlement`, `relation`, `frame`) */;
>   status: 'met'|'partly'|'unmet'|'pending' /* the verification report's row (V13 §3.1 (7)); `pending` before its verifying stage ran */; verifiedBy?: StageId; reason?: Id /* the reason record of the verification (`whyCell`'s form: "arid at 180 mm/yr in the Greyspine's shadow") */ }
> interface SettlementFacts { hostKey: string; tier: Rung; population?: number; terrain: string /* the host's terrain word through the adapter folder's terrain table → a relief class, a coast flag and side, a river flag (DR1's row) */; access: string /* its route word through the adapter folder's route table → coast, river, road, crossroads, isolation */;
>   climateRow: string /* V11's `ClimateRow`, from the terrain word by DR42's rule (the dossier carries no climate field of its own) */; latitudeBand?: [number, number] /* from `site.culture` (DR29) where the host sends a culture */; resources: string[]; lake?: boolean; coastSide?: 'N'|'E'|'S'|'W'; riverInflow?: boolean }   // the included settlements' facts (`CampaignFacts`, V13 §3.11.6; H31), each fact a rank-1 requirement of kind `terrain`, `coast`, `river`, `lake`, `climateRow`, `latitude`, `resource` or `route`
> interface CampaignFacts { campaign: string; version: number; settlements: SettlementFacts[]; relations: { a: string; b: string; word: string /* the host's relation word through the adapter folder's relation table: partner, rival, overlord, vassal, ally, hostile, isolated */ }[] }   // the port's recorded input for "based off of my settlements" (V13 §3.11.6): a windowed replace keyed by host keys
> interface Conflict { id: Id; requirement: Id; action: EditRef | 'generate' | 'placeFrame' | 'physicsPass' | 'reroll'; rankOfAction: Rank; rankCrossed: Rank /* the higher rank the action would change: 1 a dossier fact, 2 an override or a lock (V13 §3.11.5 (1)) */;
>   settlements: string[] /* every affected settlement — one stroke may touch several */; what: { field: string; from: unknown; to: unknown }[] /* the facts that would change: a dossier's climate row, a locked region's cells, an override's value */;
>   choices: ConflictChoice[] /* the choices this conflict admits: `moveTown` never under `hostLocked`; `changeRequest` at generation only; `updateDossier` only where a dossier fact is crossed */;
>   preview: { dossierFields: string[]; hostEconomyFelt: boolean | 'unknown' /* whether the host's economy or food would feel it, read from the host's own exposure words when sent (H32), else unknown */; settlementMap: 'nothing' /* the settlement map does nothing by itself: its surroundings read the new land cover at view time; regenerating the town's own ground is a separate, explicit act (`worldWins`) */ } }
> type ConflictChoice = 'cancel'|'fitWorld'|'paintAround'|'keepMismatch'|'updateDossier'|'moveTown'|'changeRequest';
> interface Decision { id: Id; conflict: Id; requirement: Id; choice: ConflictChoice; kind?: 'landChanged'|'alwaysSo' /* `updateDossier`'s two kinds: a dated host event from this week (the default; the old biome kept in the host's history) or the host's retcon path under its own guards */;
>   by: 'user'|'preset' /* a preset pre-decides map-side choices only: `updateDossier` is never `by: 'preset'` (J-V13.122) */; week: Week; restsOn: Hash64 /* the hash of what the decision rested on — the settlement's facts, the dossier's version, the area's lock state, the requirement's target — re-asked when it moves (V13 §3.11.5 (1)) */; hostSeq?: number /* an `updateDossier` carried out: the host's confirmation (`dossierUpdated`); absent while `W_DOSSIER_PENDING` stands */ }
> interface VerificationReport { run: { seq: number; goal: 'generate'|'leastChange'|'arrange'; rung: 'coarse'|'fine' }; rows: { requirement: Id; status: 'met'|'partly'|'unmet'; by: StageId; reason: Id }[]; backSteps: { from: StageId; to: StageId; cause: Id /* the requirement */; op: number /* the correction's op index */ }[];
>   unmet: { requirement: Id; offer: 'largerWorld'|'widerLatBox'|'moreRelief'|'paint'|'none' }[] /* a requirement that cannot be met at the asked size says so and offers the fix; the run never fakes the result (V13 §3.1 (7)) */; conflicts: Id[] }   // written to `derived` at every run's end: the world dialog's "what was done" and the clerk's "why this desert"
> interface SolveResult { ops: RecipeOp[] /* the corrections, `origin: 'solver'`, each naming its requirement */; placements?: Placement[] /* `leastChange`: the three offers; `arrange`: the member placements */; report: VerificationReport; conflicts: Conflict[] /* shown before the run commits: the run pauses at an approval point and continues after `decide` */ }
> declare function solve(requirements: Requirement[], state: WorldDocument | { members: Placement[] } | null, goal: 'generate'|'leastChange'|'arrange', opts?: { rung: 'coarse'|'fine'; decisions: Decision[]; seed: Seed }): SolveResult;   // @plat/world/solve: the pipeline's one entry (V13 §3.0) — never a second engine; V15 calls it with `'arrange'` over map placements (the spine's §14.5)
>
> // the DM's two verbs on every physical layer (V13 §3.14.1; the spine's §7.1; J-V13.129)
> interface Override { id: Id; layer: 'biome'|'soil'|'landCover'|'current'|'terrain' /* `terrain`: an `alwaysSo` `setField` stroke is already the base's — listed so one record form names every always-so paint */; cells: Uint32Array /* fine (biome, soil, landCover), coarse (current) */; value: number | { u: number; v: number } /* the class id, or the current vector in the raster's unit */;
>   since: { seq: number; week: Week }; released?: { seq: number; week: Week; keptAsHistory: boolean } /* a physics pass's `release`: the override no longer binds; kept as a visual trace or removed, the DM's choice */; reason: Id }   // an always-so override: a boundary condition of every dirty set that touches its cells, never overwritten by a re-derivation (V13 §3.14.2); rank 2 of the ladder
> interface Disturbance { id: Id; kind: 'fire'|'flood'|'clearing'|'eruption'|'ashfall'|'lavaFlow'|'razing'|'plague' /* the cover law's event kinds (`world.cover`); `razing` and `plague` arrive as host events the DM attributes */; cells: Uint32Array /* fine */; week: Week; severity: number /* 0–1 */;
>   source: 'user'|'host' /* a DM's stamp, or a host `WorldEvent` the DM attributed — never by itself, as a host flood raises no surface by itself (V13 §3.11.6) */; event?: Id /* the `WorldEvent` */; reason: Id }   // a this-happened disturbance: dated, the land recovers by the succession table from its week (V13 §3.6 (5)); never undone by physics, never erased by a reroll
> interface CoverPlate { seq: number /* the lens window's, or the disturbance's event seq */; week: Week; cover: Uint8Array /* fine; `world.cover`'s class per land cell: the potential vegetation's own class where unused and undisturbed, else field, pasture, coppice, fallow, scrub, regrowth, young forest, burn scar, flood silt, ash, lava, bare */;
>   eventWeek: Uint16Array /* fine, sparse-coded: the cell's last dated event's week (0 none) */; eventKind: Uint8Array /* fine, sparse-coded */; key: boolean /* a whole plate; else a delta on the previous kept plate */; pinned: boolean }   // the cover cache (`Document.coverPlates`, BCR-V13-41): one per host advance and per dated disturbance, outside every digest, re-derivable from the recipe and the event log, read by no stage (V13 §3.6 (5), §5.8 (9))
>
> // formation's entities and knobs (V13 §3.2.1; J-V13.107 – J-V13.111)
> interface Landform { id: Id; kind: 'range'|'volcano'|'caldera'|'rift'|'fault'|'hotspotChain'|'glacialLake'|'fjord'|'cirque'|'plateau'|'basin'|'trench'|'arc'|'ria'|'raisedBeach'|'suture'|'escarpment'; outline: WorldPointCm[][] /* rings in world cm (the formation grid's cells traced) */; crest?: WorldPointCm[] /* a range's, rift's, arc's or escarpment's spine */;
>   heightM?: number /* the peak, or the depth */; formed: { scenario: string; feature: 'boundary'|'hotspot'|'relict'|'glacial'|'seaLevel' } /* the bake's provenance in words, so the clerk can say "a collision range of the young-range scenario"; no block state */; active?: boolean /* a volcano or a fault the DM marks active: a hazard reading the host may read (`SettlementPhysical.hazards`); an eruption is a `Disturbance` the DM stamps, never a roll */;
>   name?: NameRef /* W9's `range` kind, now with a producer */; reason: Id }
> interface FormationKnobs { blocks: number /* the crust blocks on the formation grid (the continent rung's model; 1–2 at the realm rung as a regional context; 0 at the province rung, which takes one regional feature) */; continentalShare: number /* 0–1 */; speedMmYr: [number, number] /* the blocks' relative speeds, drawn in the range */; ageMyr: number /* the orogeny's age: a young range stands high and sharp, an old one worn */;
>   relictSutures: number; iceExtent: number /* 0–1: the glacial maximum's reach, an ELA depression scaled */; seaLevel: { glacialM: number; stands: number } /* the sea-level history: the glacial low and how many stands */; drama: number /* a multiplier on the boundary density, 1 Earth's */; hotspots: number }   // the five shipped scenarios' knob ranges are `world.formation`'s rows (V13 §2.3); W0 draws each on `world.recipe` unless the dialog set or locked it
>
> // the seams owed to V15 (the spine's §14; J-V13.127)
> interface WorldEdgeCharter { version: string; world: Uuid; side: 'N'|'E'|'S'|'W'; span: { fromCm: WorldCm; toCm: WorldCm } /* the stretch of the edge shared */; stationCm: Cm /* one station per coarse cell along the span */;
>   stations: { atCm: WorldCm; demCm: Cm /* the fine DEM's mean at the station */; bedrock: number; soil: number; biome: number /* the potential natural vegetation, graded by the neighbour's own ecotone */; water: 'land'|'sea'|'lake'; tableCm: Cm;
>     monthly: { tempDeciC: Int16Array; precipMm: Uint16Array; windCdeg: Uint16Array; windStrength: Uint8Array; moisture: Uint16Array /* the march's moisture arriving at the station per month — how a rain shadow continues */; maritime: Uint8Array } }[];
>   riverCrossings: { reach: Id; atCm: WorldCm; direction: 'in'|'out'; dischargeM3s: M3s; bedCm: Cm }[]; landforms: { kind: Landform['kind']; crest: WorldPointCm[]; heightM?: number }[] /* those that run off the edge */;
>   currents?: { psi: Float32Array /* the stream function along an open-sea span, so net transport is continuous */ }; overrides: { layer: Override['layer']; atCm: [WorldCm, WorldCm]; value: Override['value'] }[] /* the neighbour's always-so overrides along the edge — true at every time by definition */;
>   latBox: { southDeg: number; northDeg: number }; datum: 'sea0'; neighbourVersion: string /* the neighbour's model digest at emission */; reason: Id }   // `FrameCharter`'s sibling one rung up: TIMELESS facts only — the test is "would this fact be identical at every week?" (T-V13-68); it NEVER carries a this-happened event, a land-cover class, a dated surface, a plate or any simulation state; produced by `edgeCharter(world, side, span)`, consumed by W0 as locked requirements (V13 §3.1 (6)); V15 only carries it (the spine's §14.1)
> interface RenderedWorld { doc: Uuid; week: Week /* the week drawn: the world's present unless asked otherwise */; presentWeek: Week; dateLabel: string /* the host's calendar words where bound, else the season control's */; rung: WorldSizeRung; extentCm: { w: WorldCm; h: WorldCm }; datum: 'sea0'; latBox: { southDeg: number; northDeg: number };
>   tiles: { stop: 0|1|2|3; set: Id /* the §8.1 tile set at that stop for the audience */ }[]; known: { audience: 'dm'|'public'|`party:${string}`; cells: Uint32Array /* coarse: the cells the audience's places have reached (V14's layers through the world's `knownTo`) */ }[] }   // `renderAt(world, week, audience)`'s reading — tiles, extent, datum, latitude box, rung, present week, date label and the known sets per audience — and nothing else (the spine's §14.2); a view read, never persisted
>
> // sail routing (V13 §3.8 (12); J-V13.125)
> interface SailingLeg { route: Id; vessel: 'sail'|'oared'|'coastal'; month: number; direction: 'out'|'back'; effortM: number /* integer sea effort-metres: Σ over the laid course of the segment's length ÷ its ground-speed share (the polar at the wind angle, + the current's along-track share, − leeway, + the tacking penalty) */;
>   course: WorldPointCm[]; tacks: number; closed: boolean /* the month set's risk at its cap */; reason: Id }   // a pure function of the live wind and current fields and the coast; no random weather (storms stay the host's storm season); cached in `derived` per (lane, geometry version, climate version), never a digest
*Evidence:* the spine's §4.1 (the requirement record's fields and sources), §4.5 (a requirement that cannot be met "says so and offers the fix"), §5.3 (the conflict's choices and preview), §5.4 (presets and memory), §7.1 (overrides and disturbances), §10.4 (the edge charter's field list), §14.1–§14.5; the science note's §6.3 (the closed-form state function), §6.4 (the recovery kinds), §1.5 (the controllable parameters: plate count, continental fraction, speeds, hotspots, relict sutures, sea-level datum, the drama multiplier), §8.6 (the routing method); the state note's A.11–A.14, A.16, D.8 (the host's dossier fields "based off of my settlements" would read), E (v), (ix).

> interface FormationRule {                                                                                   // world.formation — the formation op (V13 §3.2.1); every cell {value, unit, provenance, calibratable}

>   blocks: { seedSpacingShare: number; growthJitter: number; closingMmYr: number; shearMmYr: number; maxSpeedMmYr: number; phantomMarginShare: number } /* the crust-block model: blue-noise seeds, the randomized flood growth, the boundary classification thresholds, the phantom-plate margin (V13 §3.2.1 (1)–(4)) */;
>   crust: { continentalKm: number; oceanicKm: number; ridgeDepthM: number; depthAgeCoefM: number; shelfBreakM: number } /* isostasy and the ocean floor's depth by age */;

>   erosion: { m: number; n: number; kByBedrock: Record<string, number>; iterations: number; channelInitCells: number; talusDegByBedrock: Record<string, number>; talusPasses: number; precipWeighted: boolean } /* the steady-state stream-power fixed point and the talus relaxation (V13 §3.2.1 (6)) */;
>   glacial: { lgmOffsetDeciC: number; elaByLatDeg: [number, number][]; elaWetDepressionM: number; erosionExponent: number; troughWidthExponent: number; cirqueAspect: 'polar' } /* the glacial carve (V13 §3.2.1 (7)) */;
>   seaLevel: { glacialLowM: number; reboundShare: number; reboundTauYr: number; riaBandKm: number } /* the sea-level stands (V13 §3.2.1 (7)) */;
>   bedrockToGeology: Record<string, 'ordinary'|'soluble'|'volcanic'|'impermeable'>; surfaceAgeByLandform: Record<string, number> /* log-years */; version: string /* `formation.version`: bumped by any change to the formation code or these laws' meaning; a stored payload is never re-run (J-V13.111) */ }
> interface SoilRule { rows: { id: string; label: string; order: number; rule: string /* the first-match decision list's row in the volume's expression form: thresholds on MAT, MAP, the aridity index, slope, the wetland flag, groundwater depth, permafrost, the parent class and the surface age (V13 §3.2.1 (9)) */; fertility: number; tillage: 'easy'|'hard'; drainage: 'free'|'moderate'|'poor'; colour: Color }[];
>   aridity: { hyperArid: number; arid: number; semiArid: number; drySubHumid: number } /* the aridity index bands (MAP ÷ PET) */; deposits: { loessDownwindKm: number; ashRadiusKm: number } }   // world.soil — the formation op's soil step; twelve classes
> interface KoppenRule { cdBoundaryDeciC: number /* the temperate/cold boundary: 0 (the published variant this volume takes) */; thresholds: { tropicalColdMonthDeciC: number; polarHotMonthDeciC: number; tundraHotMonthDeciC: number; hotSummerDeciC: number; warmSummerMonths: number; coldSummerMonths: number; veryColdWinterDeciC: number; drySummerMm: number; drySummerRatio: number; dryWinterRatio: number; rainforestDryMm: number; monsoonBase: number; monsoonDivisor: number; aridFactorWinter: number; aridFactorSummer: number; aridFactorEven: number; aridMultiplier: number; desertMultiplier: number; hotAridDeciC: number } /* every threshold of the three-letter classification as data (V13 §3.6 (1)) */ }   // world.koppen — W5
> interface PnvRule { rows: { koppen: string /* a class pattern: `Af`, `Am`, `Aw`, `BWh`, `BWk`, `BSh`, `BSk`, `Cs*`, `Cw*`, `Cfa`, `Cfb`, `Cfc`, `Dfa`, `Dfb`, `Dwa`, `Dwb`, `Dfc`, `Dfd`, `Dwc`, `Dwd`, `ET`, `EF` */; biome: string /* a `world.biomes` row id */; dryMargin?: string /* the biome at the class's dry margin (a forest-steppe) */ }[];
>   overrides: { treelineGrowingSeasonDeciC: number; growingSeasonMonths: number; wetlandAccumCells: number; wetlandSlopeMax: number; riparianCells: number; riparianAridClasses: string[]; edaphic: { soil: string; biome: string }[] } }   // world.pnv — W5's potential natural vegetation (V13 §3.6 (1))
> interface CoverRule { use: { landPerPersonHaByTier: Record<Rung, number>; cropShare: number; fallowShare: number; pastureShare: number; woodlandShare: number; catchmentHours: number; fieldSlopeMaxPct: number; fertilityMin: number; walkKmH: number; slopeFactor: number } /* human use: the area demand per seat from its population and tier, assigned by travel time (V13 §3.6 (5) (a)) */;
>   succession: { biome: string; stages: { cover: string; fromYears: number }[] }[] /* the regrowth sequence after abandonment per biome (V13 §3.6 (5) (b)) */;
>   recovery: { kind: Disturbance['kind']; biome: string; stages: { cover: string; fromYears: number }[]; soilReset?: string }[] /* the recovery sequence per disturbance kind and biome (V13 §3.6 (5) (c)) */;
>   plates: { fullWeeks: number; compactWeeks: number; keyEvery: number; storeMbGz: number } /* the cover cache's horizon, as the lens plates' */ }   // world.cover — the cover fold (`WORLD_COVER`)
> interface SailingRule { vessels: { kind: 'sail'|'oared'|'coastal'; polar: number[] /* the speed share by true-wind angle off the bow at 0°, 45°, 60°, 70°, 90°, 120°, 150°, 180° (interpolated); 0 a course the rig cannot hold */; fairKn: number /* the fair-wind speed */; oarKn?: number; leewayDeg: number; tackPenaltyMin: number; wearPenaltyMin: number; maxDaysFromShore?: number; nightStop: boolean; coastalFactor: number /* the coastal average against open water */ }[];
>   currentCoupling: number /* the share of the current's along-track component added to the ground speed, 1 */; routingGridKm: number; headings: number; months: { closedRisk: number[] /* per month: the risk multiplier of a closed sea at its cap (a cost, never a ban; the host's storm season stays the host's) */; windowRisk: number[] } }   // world.sailing — W7's sea legs (V13 §3.8 (12))
> interface SolverRule { maxBackSteps: number /* the bounded back-step from W3 to W1 (V13 §3.0) */; backStepRung: 'coarse'; causes: ('rangeUpwind'|'basinNoOutlet'|'coldCurrentCoast'|'narrowedSea'|'latBoxWiden'|'wetnessShift'|'landShareShift')[] /* the smallest-cause candidates, scored in this order (V13 §3.1 (5)) */;
>   minExtentCells: Record<WorldSizeRung, number> /* the smallest coarse extent an organic correction makes at the rung — no arid dot (V13 §3.11.1) */; gradeBandCells: Record<WorldSizeRung, number> /* the graded edge's width in coarse cells (steppe and scrub between desert and green) */;
>   protectRadiusKm: number /* every other settlement's facts protected within it: a correction never changes a cell whose change would contradict another placed or included settlement's requirement */; offers: number /* the best form's offers, 3 */; previewRung: 'coarse'; paintLastResort: boolean /* painting only when the DM forbids terrain change, labelled `W_SOLVER_PAINTED` */ }   // world.solver
> interface PresetRule { options: { key: string; group: 'basic'|'advanced'|'deep'; stage: StageId /* the earliest stage the option affects: a change re-runs from it */; kind: Requirement['kind']; default: 'random'; rerollable: boolean }[] /* the surface's options with their groups and stages (V13 §3.1 (1); V09 §4.22) */;
>   presets: { id: string; label: string; choices: OptionChoice[]; decisions: { choice: ConflictChoice; when: 'fitAlways'|'keepFlagged'|'moveUnlocked' }[] /* map-side pre-decisions only: never `updateDossier` (J-V13.122) */ }[] }   // world.presets — the named presets; a user's preset is an `editPreset` row (§7.6)

> | `belts.itczDeg` / `itczSwingOceanDeg` / `itczSwingLandDeg` / `subtropicalOffsetDeg` / `polarFrontDeg` / `lagMonths` / `landFetchKm` | 6 / 7 / 22 / 30 / 60 / 1 / 3 000 | the annual-mean convergence zone sits north of the equator; it swings 5–10° over ocean and to 20–25° over a large continent, a month behind the sun; the belt edges follow it; the swing a column takes grows with its land fraction within `landFetchKm` of it (V13 §3.4 (5)) — anchors PLAUSIBLE, the swing orders CONFIRMED |
> | `thermal.onshorePerDeciC` / `monsoonReversalDeg` / `wetHalfShare` | 0.004 / 120 / 0.7 | the land–sea thermal term: an onshore component per deci-degree of land–sea contrast in the warm half-year, offshore in the cold; a cell reads monsoon where its wind reverses by more than 120° between the two solstice months and the wet half-year holds more than 70 % of its rain — at the continent rung emergent, below it only through `worldContext.monsoon` (V13 §3.4 (5); `W_MONSOON_CONTEXT`) |

### Volume 13 — §2.5 Identity: lineage units, creation keys and the namespace segment

**956.** **Creation keys for the landforms, the overrides, the disturbances and the solver's records.**
*Requests:* F1, F3, F6–F9.
*Current text:*
> | a `ReasonRecord` | the fact it explains | (`reason`, the fact's lineage, the path or `''`, the writing event's `seq`) |
*New text:*
> | a `ReasonRecord` | the fact it explains | (`reason`, the fact's lineage, the path or `''`, the writing event's `seq`) |
> | a `Landform` | the world root | (`landform`, its kind, the formation cell of its outline's lowest-then-leftmost vertex) — so a re-bake under a new scenario draw mints new landforms and a `weather` op finds the old ones again where they stand |
> | an `Override` | the event | (the event's `seq`, its ordinal) — user-created entities of their event (§6.3), as traces are |
> | a `Disturbance` | the event | (the event's `seq`, its ordinal); a host-attributed one also names the `WorldEvent`'s key in its reason |
> | a `Requirement` | the world root | (`requirement`, its source, its kind, the hash of its target and scope) — so the same settlement's same fact is one requirement across runs, and a decision keyed to it survives a reroll |
> | a `Conflict` | the world root | (`conflict`, the requirement's lineage, the action's `seq` or the run's) — derived, re-minted per run |
> | a `Decision` | the world root | (`decision`, the requirement's lineage, the `restsOn` hash) — a decision whose basis moved is a new entity, the old one kept as history |
> | a `CoverPlate` | — (a cache, never an entity: keyed by its window's `seq` or its disturbance's event `seq`, as a `LensPlate` is) | — |
> | a `SailingLeg` | — (derived, never an entity: keyed by (route, vessel, month, direction) in `derived`) | — |
*Evidence:* V13 §2.5's own rule for user-created entities (traces); the spine's §5.4 ("Every decision is stored with its requirement … asked anew only when what it rested on changed").

### Volume 13 — §2.7 The version table, the digests and the storage of the world

**958.** **The baked payload's chunks, the cover cache, the solver's records and the share code's payload hash.**
*Requests:* F1, F2, F9; V13 §11 Q3 (the spine's §3; the state note's E (ii)).
*Current text:*
> surfaces (a few records), inside §2.9's size budget.
*New text:*
> surfaces (a few records), inside §2.9's size budget.
>
> **The formation payload** (J-V13.111). A `formation` op's `baked` results are `fields` chunks of the world — the
> formation-grid DEM as a second-order delta-coded Int32 stream (≈ 0.6 B per formation cell: ≤ 350 KB at the continent
> rung), `bedrock`, `soil` and `surfaceAge` run-length coded (≤ 200 KB together) — referenced from the op by content
> hash, as the `image` op references its asset through `packs`; the `Landform` records sit in the entities block (≤ 200
> at the continent rung). They are model data and sit in the model digest as every raster does (two worlds baked alike
> hash alike; a world re-derived from its recipe reads them and never re-runs the op, so its digest never moves when
> the formation code moves — T-V13-57). A `weather` op's delta is stored the same way. **The share code** (V13 §11 Q3)
> replays W0–W9 from `Recipe.world` as before; where the recipe's first op is `formation` the code carries the
> payload's chunk hashes, and the chunks are re-fetched from the store the code names — the host's per-campaign world
> record, the gallery's stored snapshot or the user's file — never re-run: a code whose chunks cannot be fetched refuses
> with the hash named (`refuse.world.formationPayload`), since a re-run under a later `formation.version` could differ
> and a share code must replay byte-identically or not at all. The world document's budget grows by the payload (≤
> 0.6 MB gzipped at the continent rung, inside `docMb`).
>
> **The cover cache** (`Document.coverPlates`, BCR-V13-41) is the plate cache's sibling: outside the model digest, the
> records digest, the canon and every export's model; kept across loads and saves; delta-coded with a whole plate every
> `cover.plates.keyEvery`; compacted past `cover.plates.fullWeeks` as the lens plates are; budgeted apart (`WORLD-17`).
> Unlike a lens plate it is **re-derivable** — a pure function of the potential vegetation, the dated host facts the
> windows carry and the disturbances in the entities block — so a dropped cache rebuilds at the next view and no
> reading of it is ever the only copy of a fact. **The solver's records** — `Recipe.world.choices`, `requirements` and
> `decisions` — are inputs and sit in the recipe (the model digest, as every model-affecting input does); the
> `VerificationReport`, the `Conflict`s and the `SailingLeg`s are derived and sit in `derived`, outside both digests.
*Evidence:* the spine's §3 (the payload rides the recipe; a saved world never re-runs formation), §15 (M0 untouched); the state note's A.2 (J-V13.6: the op list the one input), A.20 (Q3 and the payload), E (ii), (xv); the science note's §11 (the formation grid's cell counts).
